[[P32]]

parts of the Kaṭha Āraṇyaka contain only a description of the Pravargya without reference to the Agniṣṭoma. The original status of the Pravargya as an independent rite seems to be reflected here. The Pravargya as belonging to the Soma sacrifice, is referred to only in the third and (as it seems) last chapter of the Kaṭha-Āraṇyaka⁵⁹.

### 5 Taittirīya Āraṇyaka 5 and its relation to the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa and Saṃhitā

The Brāhmaṇa of the Pravargya ceremony of the Taittirīya śākhā of the Yajurveda is prapāṭhaka or book 5 of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka,⁶⁰ divided into 12 anuvākas (chapters) and 108 daśatis or decades of lines. The Pravargya mantras are TĀ 4. Much of the TĀ is probably comparatively old, though many parts show signs of a late (secondary) redaction.⁶¹ As far as linguistic and stylistic peculiarities are concerned, the TĀ Pravargya portions follow the TS,⁶² For example, the form *ajakṣiréṇa* in TĀ 5, 3, 9 is as it appears in the TS, where there is a "clear tendency to shorten the long

59 Another indication of an originally independent Pravargya, is discussed in note 11 to the translation.

60 Thus according to the Andhra recension (see below) to which Sāyaṇa's commentary is attached. In the Dravida recension the same Prapāṭhaka comes eighth. Bhaṇṭa-Bhāskara's commentary follows the latter recension. On the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka in general see: Rajendralala Mitra's introduction to his edition of the text (Calcutta, 1872, see under TĀ (b)); Keith, TStransl, Introduction, p. lxxviii-lxxxv; Gonda, *Vedic* *Literature*:429-430.

61 For a survey of the contents of the Āraṇyaka and its relation to the Saṃhitā and Brāhmaṇa, see Keith, TStransl*,* Introduction, p. lxxviii-lxxxv. N. Sen ("Verbforms", 1975) suggests three major stages of development of the books of the Āraṇyaka to be distinguished: the oldest group is formed by books 2-6 (so it includes the Pravargya sections); the second stage is represented by books 1 and 7-9; the last stage is book 10 (Mahānārāyaṇa-Upaniṣad).

62 For a discussion of the language and style of the TS see Keith, *TStransl,* Introduction, p. cxl-clix.

[[P33]]

vowels of the first parts of words in composition” (Keith, *TStransl.,* Intr.p.cxliv). Further, there are several formulas held in common with the TS. For instance, the rare formula *púrvam* evóditám, úttareṇābhí *gṛṇāti,* occurring several times in our text (TĀ 5, 6, 6; 8, 3; 9, 10) is also in TS 5, 1, 4, 4.

In his study of the use of verse-repetitions in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, Brāhmaṇa and Āraṇyaka (in Proceedings of the All-India Oriental Conference, 28th session, Karnatak University, nov. 1976: 233-244), C. G. Kashikar arrives, as far as the Āraṇyaka is concerned, at the conclusion that "the entire *Taittirīya Āraṇyaka* was compiled at a much later date" than the Saṃhitā. The "compilers of the *Taittirīya* texts have generally recorded as pratikapatha a verse at its second and subsequent occurrences," although "there are a number of exceptions to this rule which cannot be looked upon as mere accidents of the tradition."

However, the facts that have been collected to form the basis of this conclusion are the following: there is variation in the order of contents of the TĀ: the Andhra tradition (with Sayana's commentary) has ten prapāṭhakas, the Draviḍa tradition eight prapāṭhakas in a different order.

There seems to have existed even a third, Karṇāṭaka tradition.⁶³ There are exceptions to the rule that the complete citations precede a *pratikapāṭha,* but this would be, in the view of the absence of a fixed order of its contents, of no great significance. In TĀ 1, 27, 6 occurs a *pratīka,* but the corresponding complete verse is not present in the TS nor in the TB. A complete citation is found in the Apastamba Mantrapatha (2, 4, 3). This would indicate, according to Kashikar, that this part of the Āraṇyaka is not older than this comparatively late text (Proc. *All-India* Or. Conf*.,* 28th session, (1976): 243).

63 See on the arrangement of the text in several manuscripts also K. Madhava Krishna Sarma, "A Note on the Text of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka”, ALB 3, part 2 (1939):

68-72*.*

[[P34]]

Now the above mentioned points, looked at carefully, permit only of the much weaker conclusion that the order of contents was not fixed for a long time and perhaps had never been fixed. There is no reason to assume a "comparatively late composition" *(Proc*. All-India Or. Conf., 28 (1976): 242) of the whole Āraṇyaka.⁶⁴ That the order was not fixed is reflected in the existence of the two recensions of the TĀ that are current today. There are also different orders for studying or reading the Taittirīya texts according to the Grhya Sūtras. First, there is the reading in which the text follows the order generally known: the text is divided into Saṃhitā, Brāhmaṇa and Āraṇyaka. This is called the Sarasvatapatha. Second, there is the division of the Taittirīya Yajurveda into five Kāndas: the Prājāpatya, Saumya, Agneya, Vaiśvadeva and Svāyambhuva Kānda.

64 (a) The opinion of the Sūtra authors on the relations between Saṃhitā, Brāhmaṇa and Āraṇyaka are of some importance in addition to the internal etxtual evidence. Their works are the earliest sources after the main Taittirīya Yajurveda texts themselves. In an earlier article (see note 68), Kashikar arrives at the conclusion that, in general, the Saṃhitā, Brāhmaṇa and Āraṇyaka enjoyed equal authority from the part of the authors of the Taittirīya Śrauta Sūtras.

(b) Keith contradicts himself in his introduction to the translation of TS: "The Pravargya sections (Āraṇyaka, iv and v) seem also to be a later addition, as the parallel to the Mantras is found only in the Khila book of the *Maitrāyaṇi* (iv 9), and there is no parallel to the Brāhmaṇa at all therein. This is supported by the fact that the *Vājasaneyi* has the Mantras in books xxxvi-xxxix, a supplement, and the Catapatha *Brāhmaṇa* treats the Pravargya in its last book, together with the Upanishad, both in the Madhyandina and also apparently once in the Kāṇva recension.” (p. lxxxi-lxxxii). But that this place of the Pravargya sections need not be due to their being a late addition, appears from Keith's own words, some 40 pages further on: The Pravargya "seems in the Yajurveda tradition to have been held of too great secrecy and importance to be included in the ordinary teaching of the school rather than to be later in time or in development, for there *are* no clear traces of lateness *in* either the Mantra or the Brāhmaṇa portions of the *texts.*" (p. cxxiv-cxxv, my emphasis).

(c) For some more pieces of evidence that point to a comparitively late final redaction (*after* Patanjali's Mahābhāṣya) of the Yajurveda and other Vedic texts that contain old material, see J. Bronkhorst in *Kratylos*, 32 (1987):55-56.

[[P35]]

35 In this latter division of the so-called Arṣeyapatha, the Pravargya mantras and Brāhmaṇa form part of the second or Saumyakāṇḍa.⁶⁵ Part of the evidence, presented by Kashikar, would point at a contradictory conclusion, viz. that the Āraṇyaka has *preceded* the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa: two Pravargya mantras, cited in their complete form in the TĀ (in 4, 3 and 4, 42), are cited in *pratīka*-form in the TB. The pratīka citation in TĀ 1, 27,

6 may well have been preceded by a complete citation of the verse (which is RV 1, 89, 9) in a (lost or not yet found) Kaṭha text. The traditional attribution of the first two prapāṭhakas of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka to this Sākhā is well founded.⁶⁶ This leaves open the possibility that an important part of the Āraṇyaka is not later, or not much later, than the Saṃhitā and Brāhmaṇa, with as perhaps the only exceptions the Upaniṣadic portions.⁶⁷ The continuity between the Āraṇyaka and the Saṃhitā and Brāhmaṇa appears also from the authors of the Sūtras and of the Kāṇḍānukrama: they refer to the mantra and Brāhmaṇa portions of the Taittirīya texts without making a distinction between TS, TB and TĀ.⁶⁸ Like the texts of the TS and TB, the Pravargya portions of the TĀ seem to have taken form

1-128). The two prapāṭhakas that deal with the Pravargya belong to those parts.

Because the order of the Yajurveda was not fixed for a long time, it remains very difficult to draw conclusions regarding the relative age of different parts of what we now call the Taittirīya Saṃhitā, Brāhmaṇa and Āraṇyaka. Textual evidence tends to point to contradictory conclusions. But because the Pravargya is such an old rite, clearly referred to in the Ṛgveda (perhaps even the preparation of the clay vessel is referred to), it seems reasonable to assume that the Taittirīyas had to pay attention to this rite at an early date. The Pravargya must have formed part of the Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda recensions in the earliest times, and was on certain grounds placed in the Aranya sections.⁷²

### 6 Taittirīya Āraṇyaka 5 and its relation to other texts

Let us now briefly consider the relation of TĀ 5 with the parallel texts. From the Kaṭha śākhā we have important fragments of both the mantras and the Brāhmaṇa of the Pravargya.⁷³ The Maitrāyaṇiyas have preserved

("Finally, the Pravargya, an essential portion of the 'recent' White Yajus... has hardly anything corresponding to it in the Black Saṃhitās: some time was needed for such a rite to make its way up to the highest collection", Renou, *Écoles Védiques*:140f). In his articles

only the mantras: MS 4, 9. The Vājasaneyins have the mantras (VS 37-39) and a corresponding Brāhmaṇa (SB 14,⁷⁴ in the Kanva recension to be found in book 16). Sources from Brāhmaṇas attached to other Vedas than the Yajurveda are AiB 1, 18-22 and KauṣB 8, 3-7.⁷⁵ Shorter references to the Pravargya are found in PB 7, 5, 6;⁷⁶ JB 3, 126;⁷⁷ and lastly GB 2, 2, 6 (almost equal to AiB 1, 18 and 22).

The most important parallel texts are Kaṭha Āraṇyaka 2 and 3 and Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 14. The fact that the Pravargya mantras and Brāhmaṇa are placed in the Āraṇyakas of the Taittirīyas and of the Kaṭhas, and with the Vājasaneyins in the last portions of the VS and SB, should be explained by an intentional ordering of the text at some stage,⁷⁸ rather than by their being later additions. As has been argued above, there are reasons to assume that they formed part of the different recensions in the earliest times. The relation between the three texts would be, in that case, approximately parallel to the relation between the main texts of the Taittirīyas, the Kaṭhas and the Vājasaneyins. With regard to the Saṃhitās, the Kaṭhas (and the Maitrāyaṇiyas) and the Taittirīya, belonging to the Krsna Yajurveda, can be taken together as against the Vājasaneyins, who belong to the Sukla Yajurveda. While the differences in geographical, linguistic, religious, and cultural background of the Yajurveda schools “Apropos” and “Avāntaradīkṣā”, Kashikar made no use of the KaṭhĀ. At present, there is a new textcritical edition with German translation and commentary by M. Witzel (see under Abbreviations, KaṭhĀ), (According to Kashikar, “Mahāvīra":137 note 4, a Devanāgarī edition with English translation will appear as Nepal Research Publications, no. 2.)

74 Translated by J. Eggeling, *SBE* vol. 44, 1900.

75 Translated by Keith, *Ṛgveda* Brāhmaṇas, Harvard Oriental Series 25, London 1920.

76 Translated by Caland, Calcutta 1931.

77 Caland, *Das Jaiminīya-Brāhmaṇa in Auswahl*, Amsterdam 1919:251-257. Here the Pravargya myth is closely parallel to the one in SB 14, 1. It forms part of the story of Cyavana.

78 Cf. Keith, *TStransl*, Introduction, p. cxxiv-cxxv, cited in note 64(b) above; and van Buitenen, Pravargya:38, n. 118.

[[P39]]

were generally not very great,⁷⁹ the Taittirīyas seem to be more recent than the other Kṛṣṇa Yajurveda schools,⁸⁰ and the Vājasaneyins more recent than the Taittirīyas.⁸¹ M. Witzel mentions some compositional and linguistic arguments on the basis of which he assumes that the KaṭhĀ is the oldest and the SB Pravargya section the most recent. But it must be pointed out here, that several linguistically problematic or complicated mantras of the Taittirīyas are much simpler in their Kaṭha version, or contain what could be considered as later forms.⁸² In the case of *dhármasi sudharma*⁸³ the Kaṭhas stand apart with what seems to be a complete recast of the mantra. That the KaṭhĀ contains old material may be concluded from its discussion of the Pravargya without reference to a Soma sacrifice in the first chapters. Whether or not the KaṭhĀ is actually older than the other two texts the linguistic material is so limited, that on that basis no far reaching conclusions can be drawn it contrasts in several respects with the other two texts.

The earliest elaborate sources on the Pravargya rite apart from the TĀ and the other two texts, are the Śrauta Sūtras. From the Kaṭhas no Śrauta Sūtra is known to us. Most of the Yajurveda Sūtras belong to the Taittirīya school, and, as far as the Pravargya is concerned, they frequently refer to the TĀ. Of these, Baudhayana follows the Āraṇyaka most closely. The description of the Pravargya rite in the Katyāyana Śrauta Sūtra, the only Śrauta Sūtra of the Sukla Yajurveda, is rather succinct in comparison with the elaborate descriptions of Baudhāyana, Bharadvaja and Apastamba. While the TĀ Pravargya sections are often referred to in the Śrauta Sūtras, there are now and then several differences in the details of performance between the TĀ Pravargya ritual and that of

79 They all took shape in the Madhyadeśa, where they were not isolated from each other. See note 69.

80 Renou, *Écoles Védiques*:144-5.

81 Eggeling, *SBtransl* I:xxvii; Renou, *Écoles* Védiques:138, note 3).

82 Cf. note 24, 28 and 134(a) to the translation.

83 TĀ 4, 10, 2(3a); VS 38, 14fg; see note 120 to the translation.

[[P40]]

the Śrauta Sūtras. In one case there even seems to be a complete reinterpretation of a TĀ-passage.⁸⁴

### 7 About the Text and Translation

For the convenience of the reader, the Sanskrit text has been given along with the translation. There are three editions of the TĀ: C, P and M (see Abbreviations, under TĀ). None of them can be called really critical. In general they are quite similar, as far as the Pravargya mantras and Brāhmaṇa are concerned. I follow edition M, which seems to have the fewest cases of faulty or unclear printing. The text has been reproduced as precisely as possible as it appears in this edition, except for the following: `a) Sandhi. Unusual sandhis used in this edition (~-, -IE T-, -ZY T-) have been replaced by the more commonly used (-स-, -अ: स, ट् स -). b) Avagraha. All three editions, M, C and P, make use of the single and double avagraha, to indicate *pariśliṣṭa*-*sandhi* with *a* and ā, but none of them is entirely consistent. The use of the avagraha in those cases has the theoretical disadvantage that it suggests that the sandhi results from an absorption of the second vowel by the first (Malamoud, Svādhyāya:92). The avagraha has been used by me only to mark the disappearance of an initial short *a* after *e* and o, in accordance with modern usage (as e.g. in the *Śrautakośa*, Sanskrit section; cf. Whitney, Sanskrit Grammar:9, §16a and b).

c) Word division. Wherever sandhi permits the division of words, there is no reason not to show this in the text, since the "partial word division now in use in printed Devanāgarī texts is a make-shift" (F. B. J. Kuiper in his preface to Gopālakelicandrikā (Verhand. der Kon. Nederl. Akademie van Wetensch., Afd. Letterkunde, N. R., deel 134. Amsterdam-Oxford-New York, 1987), p. 6. It may seem that this make-shift is enforced by the character of the Indian script (Kuiper, loc. cit.), but in fact Devanāgarī has all instruments to separate syllables, words and word groups at whichever

84 See section 3.3 above, and note 11 and 130 to the translation.

[[P41]]

place the author, editor or scribe wishes. In a few cases in the text, the accent shows that what is given as two words, actually form one single word. In those cases I combined those two parts. After all, these irregular separations may very well originate from the editors or from late manuscripts used by them, since early manuscripts usually give no word divisions at all.

d) Punctuation. The dandas are exactly reproduced from edition M. Commas, question marks and quotation marks have been added by me. In the case of quotations of words or mantras spoken, the sandhi with the following *iti* has of course been maintained. In case this sandhi resulted in a single vowel, the end quotes appear after this vowel and before the *ti* of *íti*. Moreover, if the Brāhmaṇa cites only a part of the mantra, and if the rest has been translated in the translation, the additional parts have been added within brackets in the text too. The traditional division in daśatis has been indicated by a number between double dandas at the end of each dasati. The translation follows this division, as far as possible, and the numbers have been given between parentheses at the beginning of each daśati.

Variants appearing in the other two editions that are not obvious printing errors, have been mentioned in notes. The list of words at the end of each anuvāka, and at the end of the book, do not need to be included here. They have an important function in the transmission of the text: they should preserve the authenticity and prevent interpolations and omissions.

Like most previous scholars, I will refer in the notes to edition C: the first number indicates the prapāṭhaka, the second the anuvāka, the third the daśati; in the case of the Pravargya mantras there is a fourth number (between parentheses), which is the number a specific part of the text has in this edition. This part may consist of one or more mantras, or of a few pādas, a single pāda or less, depending on how the commentator (in this case Sāyaṇa) has divided the text in his commentary. In edition P the division is similar to the division in C, but the numbers are absent. In edition M specific parts are numbered too, but the division follows that of

[[P42]]

* the commentator Bhaṭṭa-Bhāskara. Unfortunately, there is considerable confusion in the way the Pravargya sections are referred to by different scholars. Usually three numbers are given, and the last number refers in some cases to the daśati, and in other cases to the number a specific passage or mantra has in edition C. To facilitate reference to the three editions, a concordance has been added (appendix 2).

The rest of a mantra has been translated (within square brackets: [ ]), if this prapāṭhaka of the Āraṇyaka presents only the *pratīka.* In this way a more complete picture of the ritual will arise. Moreover, the exposition of the Āraṇyaka frequently presupposes the complete mantra. Only in the cases where the addition of the other pādas of a mantra would interrupt and obscure the exposition, these pādas are given in a note. The mantras in the translation are printed in *italics*. The sequence of mantras cited in this book of the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka (the Brāhmaṇa of the Pravargya ritual) follows with a few exceptions that of the preceding book TĀ 4 (the collection of mantras of the Pravargya ritual). Therefore they can be found easily if one knows in what chapter(s) of the preceding book the mantras of a certain chapter are. This will be indicated in the introductory note at the beginning of each chapter.

References to the broad outlines of the ritual are given within parentheses () in the translation. These references are based, a) on the context in the text, b) on the commentators Bh and S, and the old Śrauta Sūtras BSS, BHSS and ApŚS, as far as they do not contradict the TĀ text. More detailed references and discussions not directly necessary for an understanding of the text and translation, are given in the notes at the end. If quotations and citations are not from an English source, they have been translated into English. For a detailed description of the sacrificial acts the reader is referred to the description of van Buitenen, *Pravargya*, and to the relevant passages in the Śrautakośa (see notes to each anuvāka).

[[P43]]

## Part II: Text and Translation

[[P45]]

### Anuvāka 1: The Pravargya as the head of the Sacrifice

This anuvāka contains the mythological story of the origin and purpose, the *purākalpa*, of the Pravargya ceremony in the form of the story of Makha. See Introduction section 3.4 and Hillebrandt, *Vedische Mythologie*:414-54; Keith, Religion:112; Rönnow, “Zur Erklärung des Pravargya etc.":113ff, esp. 127; Eggeling, *SBE* 44, p. xlvi-l; van Buitenen, *Pravargya*:16ff; Gonda, Aspects: 167ff; Kashikar, “Mahāvīra"; and Heesterman, "The Case of the Severed Head" in: Essays in *Indian* Ritual, *Kingship and* Society, Chicago 1985. In the introduction of the TĀ edition C (p. 27-28), a translation of this anuvāka is quoted from Muir, *Sanskrit* Texts IV, p. 112.

1 हरिः ॐ ।

> “शं नस् तन् नो मा हांसीत् । ॐ शान्तिः शान्तिः शान्तिः ॥ ॐ ॥

दे॒वा वै सुत्रम् आसत । ऋद्धिपरिमितं यशस्कामाः । तेऽब्रुवन् । “यं नः प्रथ॒मं यशं ऋ॒च्छात् । सर्वेषां न॒स् तत् स॒हास॒द्” इति॑ । तेषां कुरुक्षेत्रं वेदि॑र् आसीत् । तस्यै खाण्डवो दक्षिणार्ध आसीत् । तूर्ध्नम् उत्तरार्धः । परीणज् जंघनार्धः । मर उत्करः ॥१॥

।

Hari Om!

> *"Good* fortune to us... may it leave us not!” i Om peace, peace, peace. Om!

(1) The gods, indeed, desirous of glory, held a sacrificial session, ending (only) with success.

2 They said: "If glory reaches one of us first, let it be in common to us all” For them, Kuruksetra was the altar space, Khāndava was its southern part, Turghna the northern part, Parinah the rear part. The desert regions were the rubbish-heap.

(*2*) Amongst them, it was Makha Vaiṣṇava whom glory reached first. He desired that (glory) for himself. He slipped off with that (glory). Him the gods followed, because they wished to gain the glory. When he was followed, a bow came forth from his left hand, arrows from his right hand. Therefore, arrow and bow are of pure origin, for they are born from the sacrifice. 5 (3) Him, though he was alone, many (gods) could not overpower. Therefore, many without arrow and bow cannot overpower someone who has arrow and bow. He smiled (*a-smayata*). “Me, though I was alone, the many (gods) did not overpower." Brilliant energy withdrew from him who was smiling. This (brilliant energy) the gods rubbed upon some herbs. These became the Syāmākas. These are actually Smayākas (smayate: he smiles) by name. (4) That is why Smayākas are called Smayākas. Therefore, an initiate should smile (only) after covering the mouth, to preserve his brilliant energy.

He stood leaning on his bow. The white ants there said: "Let us choose a boon, then we will make this (Makha Vaiṣṇava or the sacrifice personified) subject to you. May we, wherever we dig, strike water on that place.” Therefore, wherever the white ants dig, there they strike water, (5)

That it proceeded (prāvartata*)*, that is why the Pravargya is called Pravargya. That it flew off with the sound "ghraaaam," that is why the Gharma is called Gharma. "The life-energy (*virya*) of the mighty one (*mahatah*) flew away "7 (the gods said). That is why the Mahāvīra is called Mahāvīra (6) That they collected (sam-*abharan*) him from this (earth), that is why the Samrat is called Samrat.

The gods took threefold hold of him who was spread out. Agni (took) the morning pressing, Indra the midday pressing, the All-gods the third pressing. Offering that headless sacrifice, they gained no blessings, they did not conquer the celestial world.

Those gods said to the two Aśvins: (7) “You, indeed, are the physicians. Put this head of the sacrifice back (on the body). " The two said: "Let us choose a boon: a ladlefuls must be taken for us too in this (sacrifice)” For them they took that ladleful for the Aśvins. The two put back that head of the sacrifice, the Pravargya. Offering that sacrifice united with its head, they gained blessings, they conquered the celestial world.

। यत् प्र॑व॒र्ग्यं प्रवृणक्ति॑ि । य॒ज्ञस्यैव तच् छिरः प्रति॑ दधाति । तेन सशर्णा य॒ज्ञेन । यज॑मानः । अवशिषो रुन्धे । अभि सुबर्ग लोकं ज॑यति । तस्मा॑द् ए॒ष आ॑श्व॒नप्र॑वया इव । यत् प्रवर्ग्यः ॥ ७ ॥

If one places the Pravargya vessel on the fire, then one puts back the head of the sacrifice. Offering that sacrifice united with its head, one gains blessings, one conquers the celestial world.

Therefore (because they put the head of the sacrifice on the body), this Pravargya is strong (in its mantras and sacrificial acts) for the Aśvins, as it were.

### Anuvāka 2: Collection of the materials for the Pravargya Ritual

The mantras of this anuvāka are TĀ 4, 2, 1-4, 2, 5 (16). For a description of the ritual actions see: *Śrautakośa* vol. 2, Sanskrit part 1:514-515; English part 1:28-30; 32-33; 35-36; 38-39; 41-42; 44-45; 46-47; van Buitenen, Pravargya: I, §1-§6.

सा॒वि॒त्रं जु॒होति॒ प्रसू॑त्यै । चतुर्गृही॒तेन॑ जुहोति । चतु॑ष्पादः प॒शवः॑ । प॒शून् ए॒वाव॑ रुन्धे । चतंस्त्रो दिशः । दिक्ष्व् एंव प्रति तिष्ठति ।

1 }

छन्दांसि दे॒वेभ्योऽपा॑क्रामन् । “न वो॑ भा॒गानि॑ ह॒व्यं व॑क्ष्याम॒” इति॑ । तेभ्य॑ ए॒तच् चतुर्गृही॒तम् अंधारयन् । पुरोनुवा॒क्या॑यै या॒ज्या॑यै ॥१॥ दे॒वता॑यै वषट्काराये । यच् च॑तुर्गृहीतं जु॒होति॑ । छन्दा स्य् एव तत् प्रीणाति । तान्य् अस्य प्रीतानि देवेभ्यों छन्दास्य् हव्यं वहन्ति । ब्रह्मवादिनों वदन्ति । " होतव्यं दीक्षितस्यं गृहार्इ न हो॑त॒व्या३म्” इति । ह॒विर् वै दी॑क्षि॒तः । यज् जुहुयात् । ह॒विष्कृ॑तं॒ यज॑मानम् अ॒ग्नौ प्र द॑ध्यात् । यन् न

(1) (The Adhvaryu) offers the oblation for Savitr (the Stimulator), ' for stimulation. He offers with the (clarified butter) of which four

(spoonfuls) are taken (into the ladle). Cattle are four-footed. He gains cattle. There are four quarters. He stands firm in the quarters.

The metres went away from the gods. 10 (They said:) “We, having no share, will not convey your oblation." For these (metres) they (the gods) destined the (clarified butter) of which four (spoonfuls) are taken (into the ladle), for the puronuvākyā, the vājyā, (2) the devatā and the vasat-kāra. When (the Adhvaryu) offers with the (clarified butter) of which four (spoonfuls) are taken (into the ladle), then he propitiates the meters. They, propitiated (by him), convey his oblation to the gods.

The expounders of the Veda say: "Is the (above-mentioned) oblation to be made in the house of a (sacrificer who has been) initiated, or is it not to be made?" The oblation is actually the initiate.

With the brahman-formula he approaches the head of the sacrifice. “*May* the *lord* of the *brahman proceed,*” he says. Proceeding he approaches the head of the sacrifice. *“May* the *goddess* Sūnṛtā proceed,” he says. Sūnṛtā is actually the sacrifice. "*Towards* the hero, manly, *with* fivefold munificence, "20 he says, (7) for the sacrifice is fivefold. “May the *gods* conduct the *sacrifice* for *us,*" he says. The gods he makes the conductors of the sacrifice. "O you *two goddesses*, o Heaven *and* Earth, *give me* your permission," he says. With the permission of these two he collects the head of the sacrifice. *"Today*, *may* I realize the head of *Makha,"* he says. Makha is actually the sacrifice. These requisites are five. The sacrifice is fivefold. He collects the head of the entire extent of the sacrifice. If he would procure (the ingredients) with the hide of domestic animals, he would afflict the domestic animals with suffering. He procures (the ingredients) with a black antelope skin. Only wild animals he afflicts with suffering.

Therefore, of animals that are born similar in size, (12) wild animals are smallest (at maturity), for they are afflicted with suffering.

On the hairy side he procures (the ingredients), for on this side is what is fit for sacrifice of it. They come (to the place of sacrifice) after having wrapped this (hide) round (the ingredients), for keeping back the Rakṣases. Many take (the hide with the ingredients). They pay reverence to it.²⁷ They put it down on uprooted earth, covered with sand and fenced in, for appeasement.

He mixes it with boiling water. (13) Brilliant energy he puts in it. *"*The sweet must make you *sweet,*"²⁸ he says. With the bráhman-formula he puts brilliant energy in it. If he would mix it with the potsherds from

### Anuvāka 3: The preparation of the Pravargya vessel

This section discusses the preparation of the main Pravargya vessel, together with two spare ones. According to the Śrauta Sūtras some other clay utensils are prepared now too. The mantras of this section are TĀ 4, 2, 5(17) - end, and 4,3. For a description of the ritual actions see: Śrautakośa vol. 2, Sanskrit part 1:515-516; English part 1:30-31; 33-35; 36-38; 40-41; 42-47; van Buitenen's description: I, §§7-14. A German translation of this Anuvāka is given by Rau, *Töpferei*:60-62.

With brilliant energy he brings brilliant energy to fulfilment.

1 (Baking the vessels in a fire pit:) *"For fire* you, for *the flame you, [*for *light you*, for *heat you*]*,”* he says. He puts brilliant energy in it.

38 It belongs to Varuṇa when it is surrounded by flames. With a verse dedicated to Mitra he approaches, for appeasement.³⁹ \* (Taking the vessels out of the fire pit:) "For success *you*," he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses). *"May the* god *Savitr* take you out*, [(the god) with* good hands, *with* good fingers, *with* good arms, *and* with his power], *”* he says. Only instigated by Savitr he takes it out, with the brahman-formula, together with the gods. "Without falling down on the earth, *fill* the regions and the quarters,” he says. (7) Therefore, (as) Agni (he, the Pravargya) shines brightly in all quarters.

> *“Rise up*, *be* great, *stand upright*, *you*, *be stable*, he says for firmness. \*"

He who looks at the Pravargya may indeed become blind. “I look with the *eyesight* of the sun," he says, for the protection of his eyesight. *“For the straight* one you, for the *good* one you, for secure *dwelling you*, for *prosperitity you*,*"* he says. The straight is actually this (earth). The good is the intermediate space. The secure dwelling is yonder (sun). (8) The quarters are prosperity. For him he forms these very worlds. And also (he says this) for firmness.

> * “इ॒दम् अ॒हम् अ॒मुम् आ॑मु॒ष्या॑य॒णं वि॒शा प॒शुभि॑र् ब्रह्मवर्चसेन पर्यूहामी "त्य् आह । विशैवैनं पशुभिर् ब्रह्मवर्चसेन पर्यंहति । “विशे”ति राज॒न्य॑स्य ब्रूयात् । वि॒शैवैनं पर्यूहति । “शुभि॒र्” इति॒ वैश्य॑स्य । प॒शुभि॑र् एवैनं पर्यूहति ।

> “देवं पुरश्चर सध्यासं त्वेत्य् आह । यथायर् एवैतत् ॥९॥

> *"*Now I surround so-*and*-so, descendant of so*-*and-so, *with people*, *with* cattle, with the *lustre* of *spiritual* knowledge," he says. With people, with cattle, with the lustre of spiritual knowledge he surrounds him. Let him say *"With* people" for a Ksatriya. Only with people he surrounds him. (Let him say :) *- With cattle*" for a Vaisya. Only with cattle he surrounds him.

The vessel belongs to the Asuras when nothing is poured over it.⁴¹ (9) He pours (milk) over it. He brings it among the gods. He pours goat's milk over it. Goat's milk, indeed, is the most excellent milk. The most excellent he pours over it. (He pours) with a yajus-formula⁴², for making a distinction. He pours with the metres. With metres this one is actually prepared. He pours the metres over the metres. *“Flow* over on speech*,* "he says. He gains speech. Flow over on *food,*” he says. He gains food. *“Flow* over on the *oblation,*" he says. He makes an oblation.

(With the following formula the Pravargya vessel and the other utensils, cooled down by the milk, are wrapped in the skin of a black antelope and hung up till the Pravargya will be put up and heated) “*O god,* o leader, may *I* be able to *bear* you*,*” he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses).

[[P59]]

### Anuvāka 4: Putting up and heating the Pravargya vessel

This is the first of the anuvākas discussing the regular performances of the Pravargya (anuvākas 4-8). These take place during one or more days. The mantras of this section are TĀ 4, 5. For a description of the ritual actions see: *Ś*rautakośa vol. 2, Sanskrit part. 1:531-532, English part 1:167-189; van Buitenen, *Pravargya:* II, §6-§19.

> “अनाधृष्या पुरस्ताद्” इति यद् ए॒तानि॒ यजू ष्य् आह । शीर्षत एव यज्ञस्य यज॑मान आशिषोऽवं रुन्धे । आर्युः पु॒रस्ता॑द् आह । प्र॒जां दक्षिण॒तः । प्रा॒णं प॒श्चात् । श्रोत्रम् उत्तरतः । विर्धृतिम् उपरि॑ष्टात् । प्राणान् एवास्मै समीचों दधाति । ईश्वरो वा ए॒ष दिशोऽनून्म॑दितोः । यं दिशोऽनु॑ व्यास्था॒पय॑न्ति ॥७॥ “मनो॑र् अश्वा॑सि॒ भूरिपुत्रे तमाम् अभि मृशति । "विश्वाभ्यो मा नाष्ट्राभ्यंः पाहि "] । इयं वै मनोर् अश्वा भूर्रपुत्रा । अस्याम् ए॒व प्रति॑ि तिष्ठत्य्, अनु॑न्मादाय । " सूपसद मे भूया, मामां हिःसीर्" इत्य् आहाहि सायै ।

Then, how is it that the Hotṛ does not ask for the blessings for the sacrificer ?" Indeed, one (kind of) sacrifice has the prayers for blessings before, the other has the prayers for blessings after (the chief action)*.55*

(7) When the sacrificer says these yajus-formulas (beginning with): *“(You are*) *an* invincible *(quarter*) *in the east*, so he obtains what he wishes already at the beginning of the sacrifice. (The prayer for) life he says in the east, progeny in the south, breath in the west, hearing in the north, separated maintenance above. (The sacrificer) puts the combined life-breaths in him(-self). If people make someones go round in different directions, he may indeed get confused in regard to the successive quarters. (Therefore, the following mantra is prescribed :) (8) With: *"*You are Manu's mare, *with* numerous foals *[*protect me from all dangers*],”* he touches this (earth). Manu's mare, with numerous foals is actually this (earth). On her he stands firm, to prevent confusion. *"May you* be *of* good *approach* for me, *may you* not *harm* me," he says to prevent harm.

Pravargya is actually the head of the sacrifice. Indeed, yonder Āditya (the sun) is the Pravargya. The Marutss are its rays (9) *“*Svāhā! *Surround with* the Maruts*,*” he says. He piles yonder Āditya (the sun) with rays: Hence in yonder world yonder Āditya (the sun) is surrounded with rays. Hence the king is surrounded with people. Hence the leader of a community is surrounded by his men.

When Agni was created and went forth,⁶⁰ his splendour came to the Vikankata-tree. (Therefore,) if the enclosing sticks are made of Vikarikata-wood, he gains splendour. They are twelve (10) Twelve months are a year. He gains the year. “There is a thirteenth month, "61 they say. If there is a thirteenth enclosing stick, he gains the thirteenth month with it. *“*You *are* the concealment *of the* firmament, "62 he says, for making a distinction. With: "Protect heaven from heat,” he puts the golden (disk) down, on top (of the Pravargya vessel), to prevent excessive burning of yonder (world). *disposal*; *indeed*, there *is nothing stronger than you*, *o Rudra],*"63 he says. (11) Thus he praises this (Pravargya vessel).

With: *“You* belong to *the* gāyatrī, you *belong* to *the tristubh*, *you* belong to the *jagati,*" he takes the (three) fans. With the metres he takes these. With: "Sweet, sweet, *[sweet],"* he fans. The sweet is actually prāṇa. He puts prāna in the sacrificer. They circumambulate three times (sun-wise), for prāna is threefold. They circumambulate three times, for the sacrifice has three series. (12) And also (they circumambulate three times) to ward off the Rakṣases. They circumambulate again three times (but in the opposite direction). That makes six. There are, indeed, six seasons. They stand firm in the seasons.

He who treads upon the Gharma's own body, certainly gets a bad skin. He actually treads upon the Gharma's own body, who, after having circumambulated three times, goes around a fourth time. Ugradeva Rajani 64 actually trod upon that (body) of it. (13) Thereupon, he indeed got a bad skin.

65 Therefore, after having circumambulated three times, let him not go around a fourth time, for the protection of himself.

The fans are actually the pranas. They fans without entanglement (of the fans), in order that the prāṇas do not become entangled (but) become well-ordered. Sitting down apart (around it, facing different directions) they fan. They are firmly established in the quarters. They fan upwards, to reach the celestial world. They fan in all directions. Therefore, this (wind) blows in all directions.

1 " वरु॑णस् त्वादि॒त्यैः प॒श्चाद् रोचयतु जाग॑तेन॒ छन्दसे "त्य् आह । वरु॑ण॒ ए॒वैन॑म् आदित्यैः प॒श्चाद् रो॑चयति जाग॑तेन॒ छन्द॑सा ॥१॥ “स मां रुचितो रोचयेत्य् आह । आशिष॑म् ए॒वैताम् आ शा॑स्ति ।

Agni, together with the Vasus, makes him shine in the east, with the gāyatrī metre. “Shining, make me shine !" he says. This prayer he prays.

> *“Let Indra, together with the Rudras*, make you shine in *the* south*,* *with* the *tristubh*-*metre,”* he says. Indra, together with the Rudras, makes him shine in the south, with the tristubh metre. *“Shining*, make me shine !” he says. This prayer he prays.

> *“Let Varuṇa*, *together with* the *Ādityas*, make you shine in *the west*, *with* the *jagati-metre*,” he says Varuṇa, together with the Ādityas, makes him shine in the west, with the jagati-metre. (2) *“Shining*, make me *shine*!" he says. This prayer he prays.

He shines among men. “Sovereign *king Gharma*, *you are shining* among *the gods*, *long-*lived, full of *brilliant* energy, having the *lustre* of *spiritual knowledge*,” he says, for he is shining among the gods, long-lived, full of brilliant energy, having the lustre of spiritual knowledge. "*May* I *be shining* among *men*, *long*-*lived*, full of *brilliant* energy, *having the lustre* of spiritual *knowledge*,” he says. He becomes shining among men, long-lived, full of brilliant energy, having the lustre of spiritual knowledge. '*You* are *lustre*, *put* lustre in *me*, *in me* be lustre !” he says. This prayer he prays.

If (the Adhvaryu) would proclaim: “The Gharma is shining!” without having (first) made it shine with these yajus-formulas, then the Adhvaryu would not be shining, the sacrificer would not be shining. But if he proclaims: "The Gharma is shining!" after having (first) made it shine with these yajus-formulas, the Adhvaryu becomes shining, the sacrificer becomes shining.

य॒ज्ञः । तस्य॒ शिरः प्रति॑ दधाति । नोक्थ्यें प्र वृ॑ञ्ज्यात् । प्र॒जा वै प॒शवे॑ उ॒क्थानि॑ । यद्

- (General remarks on the Pravargya ceremony:) (1) The Pravargya is actually the head of the sacrifice. The Upasad-performances are the neck.⁶⁸ Before the Upasad-performance he performs the Pravargya. He puts back the head of the sacrifice on the neck.

He performs the Pravargya three times. These worlds are three. From these very worlds he gains the head of the sacrifice. These amount to six. There are, indeed, six seasons. (2) From the seasons he gains the head of the sacrifice. He performs it twelve times. Twelve months (make a) year. From the year he gains the head of the sacrifice. These amount to twenty-four. There are twenty-four half-months. From the half-months he gains the head of the sacrifice. And then, indeed, the Pravargya is to be performed once, for the head is one.

(3) He performs it at the Agniṣṭoma.

9 The Agniṣṭoma is actually coextensive with the sacrifice. He puts back the head of that which is coextensive with the sacrifice. He should not perform it at the Ukthya

उ॒क्थ्ये॑ प्रवृ॒ञ्ज्यात् । प्र॒जां प॒शून् अ॑स्य॒ निर् र्दहेत् । विश्वजित सर्वपृष्ठे प्र वृणक्ति ॥३॥ पृष्ठानि वा अच्युतं च्यावयन्ति । पृ॒ष्ठैर् ए॒वास्मा अच्यु॑तं च्यव॑यि॒त्वाव॑ रुन्धे ।

- “अप॑श्य॑ गो॒पाम्” इत्य् आ॑ह । प्रा॒णो वै गो॒पाः । प्रा॒णम् एव प्र॒जासु वि यांतयति । “अपश्यं गोपाम्" इत्य् आह । असौ वा आदित्यो गोपाः । स हीमाः प्रजा गौपायति । तम् एव प्रजानां गोप्तारं कुरुते । “अर्निपद्यमानम्" इत्य् ओह ॥४॥ न ह्य् एष निपद्येते । “आ च परां च पथिभिश् चर॑न्त॒म्” इत्य् आ॑ह् । आ च ह्य् एष परां च पथिभिश् चरंति । “स सध्रीची: स विषूचर् वसा॑न॒” इत्य् आ॑ह । स॒धीचींश् च ह्य् एष विषूचीश् च वसानः प्रजा अभि विपश्यति । “आ व॑रीवर्त भुव॑नेष्व् अन्तर्" इत्य् आह । आ ह्य ए॒ष व॑रीवर्त भुव॑नेष्व् अ॒न्तः ।

(sacrifice). The Uktha (recitations) are actually progeny and cattle. If he would perform the Pravargya at the Ukthya, he would burn his progeny and cattle. (The priests and the sacrificer gaze at the heated Pravargya vessel:)70 *"I* saw the herdsman," he says. (First interpretation:) The herdsman is actually Prána. He distributes life (prana*)* to all creatures. “I saw the *herdsman*," he says. (Second interpretation:) The herdsman is actually yonder Āditya (the sun), for he protects these creatures (as his herd). This (vessel) he makes the protector of creatures. 71 *“Without lying down*,” he says, (5) for he does not lie down. "Wandering *hither* and *thither* on *his pathways,*” he says, for he wanders hither and thither on his pathways.

> *"Enveloping* himself in *those (*waters) *that converge*, *in those that* spread *out,*” he says, for enveloping himself in those (waters) that converge, in those that spread out, he discerns the creatures. “*He just* moves *and moves* around in *all* beings (or *in all worlds),*" he says, for he just moves and moves around in all beings (or in all worlds).

(Interpretation of this mantra:) for this Agni (the Pravargya vessel) comes together with Agni (the sun). *"Svāhā! Agni* comes *together with tapas*, *[with the god Savitr;* he *shone* together *with the* sun*],*” he says. What has been said before he praises with this subsequent (yajus-formula). *"*You shine *wide*, *bearer of* the cloudsphere of heaven, *[bearer]* of the *earth, [*bearer of *the wide firmament,* the bearing *god of the* gods, the immortal one, born from *tapas*]," he says. He forms for him the two winter-periods. "You *are renowned among* the *gods*, o *god* Gharma, protect the *gods,*” he says. He forms for him the two periods of the cool season.

"

> *"*Confer on us *speech*, born of *tapas and devoted* to the *gods,”* he says. (The speech) born of tapas is actually speech fit for sacrifice.7s This (speech) he gains. (8) "Germ of *the gods,*" he says, for he is the germ of the gods. *“*Father of *hymns, "*77 he says. The hymns are actually offspring. Of these he, the Pravargya, is the father. Therefore he says so. *“The* protector *of offspring,*” he says, for he is the protector of offspring. “The thought of the *inspired poets,*” he says, (9) for he is the thought of the inspired poets. *"With* god Savitṛ the god has *striven*, with the sun he shone,” he says. Yonder Āditya (the sun) as well as the Pravargya (vessel) he calls upon. *"You are the giver of a* full life-time to us, o *Gharma,* you are the giver *of lustre*," he says. This prayer he prays. *“*You *are our* father, *as a father awaken us*,” he says, for he awakens him. 72 *

नवैर्तेऽवकाशा भवन्ति । पर्लियै दशमः । नव वै पुरुषे प्राणाः ॥ ॥ नाभि॑िर् दश॒मी । प्रा॒णान् ए॒व यज॑माने दधाति । अथो॒ दर्शाक्षरा वि॒राट् । अन्नं॑ वि॒राट् । विराजैवान्नाद्यम् अव॑ रुन्धे । य॒ज्ञस्य॒ शिरोऽच्छिद्यत । तद् दे॒वा होत्रांभिः प्रत्य् अंदधुः । ऋत्विजोऽर्वेक्षन्ते । एता वै होत्रा: । होत्रांभिर् एव यज्ञस्य शिरः प्रति दधाति ॥१०॥ रचितम् अर्वेक्षन्ते । रुचिताद् वै प्र॒जाप॑तिः प्र॒जा अंसृजत । प्र॒जानाः सृष्टयै । रु॒चि॒तम् अवे॑क्षन्ते । रु॒चि॒ताद् वै पर्जन्यों वर्षति । वर्षुकः पर्जन्यों भवति । सं प्रजा ऐधन्ते । रुचितम् अवे॑क्षन्ते । रुचितं वै ब्रह्मवर्चसम् । ब्रह्मवर्चसिनों भवन्ति ॥११॥ अधीयन्तोऽर्वेक्षन्ते । सर्वम् आयु॑र् यन्ति । न पत्नय् अर्वेक्षेत । यत् पत्नय् अवेर्क्षेत । प्र जा॑येत । प्र॒जां त्व् अ॑स्यै निर् र्दहेत् । यन् नावेक्षैत । न प्र जा॑येत । नास्यें प्रजां निर् देहेत् । तिरस्कृत्य यजु॑र् वाचयति ।

1 These are nine Avakāśa mantras.

79 That of the wife is the tenth. Nine, indeed, are the prānasso in man. (10) The navel is the tenth. He puts the prāṇas in the sacrificer. And then: the virāj-metre is ten-syllabic. Virāj is food. With the viraj - metre he gains food.

The head of the sacrifice was cut off. That (head) the gods put back with the sacrificial acts. The priests look at (the Pravargya vessel). These are the sacrificial acts. With the sacrificial acts he puts back the head of the sacrifice. (11) They look at it when it is shining. Indeed, from what was shining Prajāpati created the creatures. (Therefore, this is) for the creation of the creatures. They look at it when it is shining. Indeed, from what is shining Pravargya rains. Pravargya becomes full of rain. Together the creatures thrive. They look at it when it is shining. The lustre of spiritual knowledge is actually what is shining. They obtain the lustre of spiritual knowledge. (12) Reciting they look at (the Pravargya vessel). They attain a full life-time.

Let the wife (of the sacrificer) not look. If the wife would look, she would bring forth, but (the Pravargya) would burn her child. If she (would be present and) would not look, she would not bring forth, (and the Pravargya) would not burn her child. (The Pratiprasthātr)

[[P74]]

### Anuvāka 7: Milking the cow and the goat

The cow and the goat are milked, the Pravargya vessel is filled with their milk and brought to the Āhavaniya fire. At the end of this anuvāka the offering of the southern Rauhiṇa cake in the Āhavanīya is dealt with. The mantras of this anuvāka are to be found in TĀ 4, 8 and 4, 9, 1/2 (1) ; the last mantra (to accompany the Rauhina oblation) is Tā 4, 10, 4(7). For a description of the ritual actions see: Śrautakośa vol 2, Sanskrit part 1:332- 534 and 535; English part 1 : 192-210 and 229-230; van Buitenen, *Pravargya*: III, 81 - IV⁸⁴, " देवस्य॑ त्वा सवितुः प्रसव" इति रशनाम् आ द॑त्ते प्रसूत्यै । “अश्विनौर् बा॒हुभ्या॒म्” इत्य् आ॑ह । अ॒श्विनो॒ हि दे॒वाना॑म् अध्वर्यू आस्ता॑म् । “पू॒ष्णो हस्ता॑भ्याम्" इत्य् आ॑ह॒ यत्यै । "आ ददे”, “ऽदित्यै रास्तासी "त्य् आ॑ह॒ यजु॑ष्कृत्यै । “इड एह्य्, अदि॑ति॒ एहि॒ सर॑स्व॒त्य् एही "त्य् आ॑ह । ए॒तानि॒ वा अ॑स्यै देवना॒मानि॑ । देवनामैर् एवैनाम् आह्वयति । " असा एह्य, असाव् एह्य्, असद् एही "त्य् ओह् । ए॒तानि॒ वा अ॑स्यै मनु॒ष्या॑ना॒मानि॑ ॥१॥ मनु॒ष्य॑नामैर् ए॒वैना॒म् आ ह्य॑यति । षट् सं पद्यन्ते । षड् वा क्रतवः । ऋतुर्भिर् एवैनाम् आह्वयति । “अदित्या उष्णीष॑म् असी"त्य् आह । यथायजुर् एवैतत् ।

(1) With: "*You* at *the impulse* of the *god Savitr”* he takes the tether, for stimulation. "*With the* arms *of* the Aśvins," he says, *for* the two Aśvins were the Adhvaryus of the gods. "*With the* hands of *Pūṣan*,*"* he says for guidance. "I *take* (you).”83 “You are the *girdle of Aditi*,” he says for the performance with the yajus-formula. *“*Iḍā come, Aditi come, *Sarasvatī* T come,” he says. These, indeed, are her divine names. By the divine names he calls her. “So-*and-*so come, *so-and*-so come, so-*and-so come*, "he says.

These, indeed, are her human names. (2) By the human names he calls her.⁸⁵ These (names) amount to six. There are, indeed, six seasons. By the seasons he calls her. "*You* are the tiara *of Aditi,*” he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses).

Like if one would say: “give milk for him,” exactly like that it is.⁸⁷ "

With: "You belong to *the* gayatri, you *belong* to the *tristubh*, you belong to the jagati," he takes the two tongs and the supporting vessel. He takes them with the metres. The two Aśvins, putting back that head of the sacrifice, said indeed: "Let the vasat-cry be uttered for us at the beginning,” *“O Indra,* o *Aśvins,* (drink *the* Gharma) of bees' *honey*, 294 he says. He utters the vasat-cry for the Aśvins at the beginning. And then he brings the Aśvins to fulfilment with a portion.

(6) *"Drink the Gharma,* o *Vasus,* offer *worship*. Vat*!”* he says. The Vasus he brings to fulfilment with a portion. If he would say "*Vasat!"* his vasat-cry would be exhausted. If he would not utter the vasat-cry, the Raksases would destroy his sacrifice. He says: "*Vat!* " He utters the vasat-cry in a veiled way. His vasat-cry does not become exhausted. The Rakṣases do not destroy his sacrifice.

(7) “*Svāhā*! *I offer⁹⁵ you* as an *oblation for* the sun's *ray* *which gains* (and *gives)*% rain," he says. The ray of him that is pure, that actually gains

1 न वा एतं मनुष्यों भर्तुम् अर्हति । “दे॒वानां॑ त्वा पितॄणाम् अनु॑मत भ शकेय॒म्” इत्य् आ॑ह । दे॒वैर् एवैनं पि॒तृभि॑र् अनु॑मत आ द॑त्ते । वि वा ए॑नम् ए॒तद् अर्धयन्ति । यत् पश्चात् प्रवृज्यं पुरो जुह्वति । "तेजोऽसि, तेजोऽनु प्रेही "त्य् आह । तेज ए॒वास्मि॑न् दधाति । “दिवि॒स्पृङ् मा मा हिसीर् अन्तरिक्षस्पृङ् मा म हिस्सी, पृथिविस्पृङ् मा मां हिस्सी" इत्या॒हाहिं सायै ॥ ॥। - सुव॑र् असि, सुर्वर् मे यच्छ, दिवं यच्छ दिवो मां पाही "त्य् आह । आशिष॑म् एवैताम् आ शांस्त । शिरो वा ए॒तद् य॒ज्ञस्य॑ । यत् प्र॑व॒र्यः । आत्मा वायुः । उद्यत्य॑ वातना॒मान्य् आ॑ह ।

(and gives) rain. To this he offers the (cow's milk). "You *are the sweet* *oblation*, 97 he says. He makes (the goat's milk) sweet (with this mantra).

> *"Heat* the heat *of* the sun,” he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses). "*With* heaven and *earth I* encompass *you*,"98 he says. With heaven and earth he encompasses it. (8) *"With* the firmament I *support you*, 99 he says. With the firmament he supports it.

A human being certainly cannot carry it. *"With* the *consent* of *gods* *and* fathers, *may* I *be* able to *carry you,*" he says. Given leave by gods and fathers he takes it. They certainly make this (Pravargya) unsuccessful, when, after having heated it in the west, they offer the oblation in the east. (To counteract this:) *“You* are *brilliant* energy, *go along* after *brilliant* energy," he says. He puts brilliant energy in it. *"Touching* heaven, may you not *harm* me; *touching* the *earth*, *may you not* harm me,” he says to prevent harm. (9) *"You are* sunlight, *give me sunlight*; give me heaven*, protect me* from *(danger* from) *heaven*," he says. This prayer he prays.

The Pravargya is actually the head of the sacrifice. The body (of the sacrifice) is the Wind.

100 After having lifted up (the Pravargya, to bring it

आत्मन्न एव यज्ञस्य शिरः प्रति दधाति । अनंवानम् । प्राणाना संतत्यै । पञ्चह ॥९॥ पङ्क्तो॑ य॒ज्ञः । यार्वान् एव यज्ञः । तस्य शिरः प्रति दधाति ।

> “अ॒ग्नये॑ त्वा वसु॑मते स्वाहेत्य् आह । असौ वा आ॑दि॒त्योऽग्निर् वसु॑मान् । तस्मा॑ एवैनं जुहोति । “सोमा॑य॒ त्वा रुद्रव॑ते॒ स्वाहेत्य् आह । च॒न्द्रमा॒ वै सोमों रुद्रवान् । तस्मा॑ ए॒वैनं जुहोति । वरु॑णाय त्वादि॒त्यव॑ते॒ स्वाहेत्य् आह ॥१०॥ अ॒प्सु वै वरु॑ण॒ आ॒दि॒त्यवा॑न् । तस्मा॑ एवैनं जुहोति । "बृह॒स्पत॑ये त्वा वि॒श्वदे॑व्याव॑ते॒ स्वाहे "त्य् आ॑ । ब्र॒ह्म॒ वै दे॒वानां॑ बृह॒स्पति॑ः । ब्रह्म॑ण॒ ए॒वैन॑ जुहोति । “स॒वि॒त्रे त्व॑र्भ॒मते॑ बिभुमते॑ प्रभुमते॒ वाज॑वते॒ स्वाहेत्य् आह । संवत्स॒रो वै स॑वि॒तर्भुमान् वि॑िभुमान् प्र॑भुमान् वाज॑वान् । तस्मा॑ ए॒वैनं॑ जुहोति । " यमाय॒ त्वाङ्गिरस्वते पितृमते स्वाहेत्य् आह । प्रा॒णो वै य॒मोऽङ्गि॑रस्वान् पितुमान् ॥११॥ तस्मा॑ ए॒वैनं॑ जुहोति । ए॒ताभ्य॑ ए॒वैनं to the Āhavanīya), 10 he says the names of the Wind. On the body he puts back the head of the sacrifice. (He recites the names) without taking breath, 12 for the continuity of the prānas. He says five names. 13 (10) The sacrifice is fivefold. The head of that sacrifice, as big as it is, he puts back.

> *"You* for *Agni*, accompanied by *the* Vasus*, svāhā!”*104 he says. Agni, accompanied by the Vasus, is actually yonder Āditya (the sun). To him he offers105 this. “You for *Soma*, *accompanied by* the *Rudras*, *svāhā!”* he says. Soma, accompanied by the Rudras, is actually the moon. To him he offers this. *“You* for *Varuṇa*, accompanied by the *ādityas*, *svāhā!”* he says. (11) Varuṇa, accompanied by the Ādityas, is actually in the waters. To him he offers this. "You for *Bṛhaspati*, *accompanied* by the All*-gods*, *svāhā!*” he says. Bṛhaspati is bráhman among the gods. To bráhman he offers this. “You for *Savitr,* accompanied by *Rbhu*, accompanied by *Vibhu*, *accompanied by Prabhu, accompanied* by *Vaja*, *svāhā!”* he says. Savitṛ, accompanied by Ṛbhu, accompanied by Vibhu, accompanied by Prabhu, accompanied by Vāja is actually the year. To him he offers this. *"*You for Yama, accompanied *by the* Angirases, *accompanied by the* fathers, svāhā!" he says. Yama, accompanied by the Angirases, accompanied by the fathers, is acturally the Prāna. (12) To him he offers

8 “अर् ज्योति॑ के॒तुना॑ जुषता: सुज्योतिर् ज्योतिषाः स्वाहा, रात्रिर् ज्योतिः केतुनां जुषताः सुज्योतिर् ज्योतिषाः स्वाहे "त्य् आह । आदित्यम् एव तद् अ॒मुष्म॑न् लो॒केऽङ्गा॑ प॒रस्ता॑द् दाधार । रात्रिया अ॒वस्ता॑त् । तस्मा॑द् असाव् आदित्योऽमुष्मिन् लोर्केऽहोरात्राभ्यां धृतः ॥१२॥

this. To these deities he offers this. (The mantras) amount to ten.

106 The viraj-metre is ten-syllabic. The virāi is food. With the virāj he gains food.

(Offering the southern Rauhiṇa-cake:)107 The gods went indeed to the celestial world by the two Rauhiṇa-cakes. That is why the two Rauhiṇas are called Rauhiṇas (√ruh-, to ascend). When the two Rauhinas are there, then the sacrificer goes to the celestial world by the Rauhiṇas.

> *“Let* day *enjoy* the lightlos by brightness; too *good light* of *lights*, *svāhā*! Let *night* enjoy the light by brightness; good light of *lights, svāhā!”* he says. It is the āditya (the sun) in yonder world that he thus supports by day above (in heaven); by night below (in the netherworld). Therefore yonder Āditya (the sun) in yonder world is supported by day and night.

[[P80]]

### Anuvāka 8: The offering to the Aśvins

This is the last of the five anuvākas that describe the regular daily performances of the Pravargya. The offering to the Aśvins and other deities is described in this anuvāka. The mantras discussed in this anuvāka are to be found in TĀ 4, 9, 2(2) - end, and 4, 10. For a description of the ritual actions see: *Śrautakośa* vol. 2, Sanskrit part 1:533-534 and 535- 536; English part 1:193-210, 229-230; van Buitenen, *Pravargya*: IV. §5-§15.

> “विश्वा॒ आशा॑ दक्षिणसद्" इत्य् आह । विश्वान् ए॒व दे॒वान् प्री॑णाति । अथो॒ दुरिष्ट्या एवैनं पाति । “विश्वा॑न् दे॒वान् अ॑याड् इहे” त्य् आह । विश्वन् ए॒व दे॒वान् भा॒ग॒धेये॑न॒ सम् अर्धयति । “स्वाहा॑कृतस्य धर्मस्य मधः पिबतम् अश्विनेत्य् आह ।

अ॒श्विना॑व् ए॒व भांगधेये॑न सम् अर्धयति । “स्वाहा॒ग्नये॑ य॒ज्ञिया॑य॒ शं यजु॑र्भिर्” इत्य् आ॑ह । अभ्य् एवैनं घारयति । अथो॑ ह॒विर् एवाकः ॥१॥ "अश्विना धर्म पांत हार्दि- वानम् अर्हर्दिवाभिर् ऊतिभिर्” इत्य् आह । अ॒श्विना॑व् एव भांग॒धेये॑न॒ सम् अर्धयति “अनुं वां द्यावापृथिवी माताम् इत्य् आहार्नुमत्यै । “स्वाहेन्द्रीय, स्वाहेन्द्रा वड्” इत्य् आ॑ह॒ । इन्द्रा॑य॒ हि पु॒रो हूयतें । आ॒श्राव्या॑ह “घ॒र्मस्य॑ य॒जे” ति । वर्षट्कृते

1 (The offering to the Aśvins:) (1) *“All quarters* (has he, the *Brahmān* priest) *who sits* in the *south (worshipped*),"110 he says. (The Brahmán priest) pleases all the gods. And he protects him (the sacrificer) from failure in sacrifice. *“*He *has worshipped the* All*-gods here*, 111 he says. The All-gods he brings to fulfilment with a portion. *"Of* the sweet *Gharma*, consecrated *with* the *Svahā,* drink, o Aśvins,” he says. The Aśvins he brings to fulfilment with a portion. "Svāhā to *Agni*, *worthy* of sacrifice*; happiness* with the *yajus-formulas,*" he says. He sprinkles him. And he makes an oblation. (2) *“O Aśvins, drink the Gharma that is dear* to *your heart*, 112 *with your favoursi* every day, 114 he says. The Aśvins he brings to fulfilment with a portion.115 "Let heaven and *earth give* you their permission,” he says, for permission. *"*Svāhā for *Indra*, svāhā *for Indra*, 116 *Vat!"* he says, because for Indra an offering is made at the beginning. After having called, he says (to the Hotṛ): "*Recite* the *yājyā* for *the Gharma*-oblation." When the vasat-cry is uttered (by the Hotṛ in his

जुहोति । रक्षसाम् अप॑हत्यै । अनु॑ यजति स्व॒गाकृ॑त्यै । " घ॒र्मम् अ॑पातम् अश्विने”त्य् आ॑ह ["हार्दवा॒नम् । अह॑र्दे॒वाभि॑रु॒तिभि॑ः "] ॥२॥ पूर्वम् ए॒वोदि॒तम् । उत्त॑रेना॒भि गृणाति ।

> “अनुं वा द्यावा॑पृथिवी अंम साताम् इत्य् आहानु॑मत्यै । "तं प्राव्यं यथावण् णमों दिवे नमः पृथिव्या" इत्य् आह । यथायजुर् एवैतत् । " दिवि धां इमं यज्ञं, य॒ज्ञम् इ॒मं दि॒वि धा॒” इत्य् आह । सुवर्गम् एवैनं लोकं गमयति । “दिवं गच्छान्तरिक्षं गच्छ पृथि॒वीं ग॒च्छे”त्य् आ॑ह । एष्व् एवैनं लोकेषु प्रति॑ि ष्ठापयति । पञ्चं प्रदिषों ग॒च्छे”त्य् आ॑ह॒ ॥३॥ दि॒क्ष्व् एवैनं॒ प्रति॑ ष्ठापयति । " दे॒वान् धर्मपान् गच्छ, पि॒तॄन् ध॑र्म॒पान् ग॒च्छे”त्य् आ॑ह । उ॒भये॑ष्व् ए॒वैनं प्रति॑ ष्ठापयति ।

recitation of the yājyā), he offers the oblation (with the first “Svāhā for *Indra*"). For keeping back the Raksases. He offers a second time (with *“Svāhā for Indra*, Vat! "), by way of svagākrti.117 *“*The *Aśvins have drunk* the Gharma *[*that is dear to *their* heart, *with* favours every *day*],” he says. (3) What has been said before he praises with this subsequent (yajus-formula).

> "Heaven and earth have *given* you their permission," he says, for the permission (received). "(Permission for) the *(Gharma*) to be *accepted* favorably, as the vat-cry. Homage to heaven*,* homage to earth,” he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses). "Place in heaven *this* sacrifice, place this *sacrifice* in heaven," he says. He makes it go to the celestial world. *“Go* to heaven, go to the firmament, go to the earth," he says, He fixes it firmly in these worlds. *“Go* to *the five* quarters,” he says. (4) He fixes it firmly in the quarters. “Go to the Gharma-*drinking gods*, *go* to the Gharma-drinking fathers," he says. He fixes it firmly among both groups.

When (the vessel) overflows,

118 Parjanya becomes rainy. Therefore, overflowing it is pure. What overflows in the eastern direction, that is for the gods. What overflows in the southern direction, that is for the 82.

म॑नु॒ष्या॑णाम् । यद् उद॑ङ् । तद् रु॒द्राणा॑म् । प्राञ्च॒म् उर्दञ्चं पिन्वयति । दे॒व॒त्राः । अथो॒ खलुं । सर्वा॒ अनु॒ दिश॑ः पिन्वयति । सर्वा दिशः सम् एधन्ते । अन्तः परिधि पिन्वयति ॥५॥ तेजसोऽस्कन्दाय ॥

> “इ॒षे पपिह्य॑ ऊ॒र्जे पो॑पि॒ही"त्य् या॑ह । इष॑म् ए॒वोर्ज॒ यज॑माने दधाति । “यज॑मानाय पीपि॒ही”त्य् आह । यज॑मानायै॒वैताम् आशिष॒म् आ शा॑स्ते । “मह्यं ज्येष्ठयां पीपिही "त्य् आह । आत्मन एवैताम् आशिषम् आ शांस्ते । "त्विष्यै त्वा, द्यु॒म्नाय॑ त्वेन्द्रि॒याय॑ त्वा॒ भूत्यै॒ त्वेत्य् आ॑ह । य॒थय॒जुर् एवैतत् । “धर्मासि सुधर्मामे॑न्य॒ अ॒स्मे ब्र॑ह्मणि धार॒ये "त्य् ओह ॥६॥ ब्रह्म॑न्न् ए॒वैनं॒ प्रति॑ ष्ठापयति । “नॆत् त्वा वार्तः स्कन्दयाद्” इति

7 यद्य् अ॑भि॒चरे॑त् । “अ॒मुष्य॑ त्वा प्रा॒णे सा॑दयाम्य् अ॒मुना॑ स॒ह ति॑र॒र्थे गच्छे" तं

fathers. (5) What in the western direction, that is for the human beings.

What in the northern direction, that is for the Rudras. He makes it overflow in the eastern and northern direction. (Thus) he brings it among the gods. But (according to others) indeed he makes it overflow in all quarters. All quarters prosper greatly. He makes it overflow inside the *paridhi*-s (sticks enclosing the sacrificial fire). (6) To prevent spilling of brilliant energy.

> "Swell for nourishment, swell for *food,*"119 he says. He puts nourishment and food in the sacrificer. *“Swell* for the *sacrificer,”* he says. For the sacrificer he prays this prayer. *"Swell* for me, for pre-eminence*,*” he says. For himself he prays this prayer. "You for energy, *you* for *splendour*, *you* for *power*, *you for prosperity,*” he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses). *"You are* a *support,* a *good support,* unoffensive *towards* us; support the *Brahmins*,"120 he says. (7) He fixes him (the sacrificer) firmly among the Brahmins. *"Lest* the wind *causes* *you to be spilt.”*

If he wants to put a spell on someone, he should say with regard to whom he hates: *“I* put *you* down on the life-breath of so-*and-so*, *go* to uselessness *together* with so*-and*-so, *[who* hates us *and whom* we *hate]*.”

ब्रूया॒द् यं द्वि॒ष्यात् [“यो॑ऽस्मान् द्वेष्टिं । यं च॑ व॒यं द्विष्मः "] । यम् एव द्वेष्टि । तेनैंन सह निरर्थ गमयति ॥

> “पू॒ष्णे शर॑से॒ स्वाहे” त्य् आ॑ह । या ए॒व दे॒वता॑ ह॒तर्भागाः । ताभ्य॑ ए॒वैनं॑ जुहोति । “ग्रावभ्यः स्वाहेत्य् आह । या एवान्तरिक्षे वाचः ॥७॥ ताभ्यं एवैनं जुहोति । “प्रति॒िरेभ्यः स्वाहे "त्य् आह । प्रा॒णा वै दे॒वाः प्र॑ति॒राः । तेभ्यं एवैनं जुहोति । " द्यावा॑ पृथि॒वीभ्यः॒ स्वाहे” त्य् आ॑ह । द्यावा॑पृथि॒वीभ्या॑म् ए॒वैनं॑ जुहोति । पि॒तृभ्यो॑ घर्म॒पेभ्यः॒ स्वाहे”त्य् आ॑ह । ये वै यज्वनः । ते पि॒तरो॑ धर्मपाः । तेभ्य॑ ए॒वैनं॑ जुहोति ॥८॥ “रु॒द्राय॑ रु॒द्रर्होत्रे॒ स्वाहे”त्य् आ॑ह । रु॒द्रम् ए॒व भा॑ग॒धेये॑न॒ सम् अ॑र्धयति । स॒र्वत॒ः सम् अनक्ति । सर्वर्त एवं रुद्रं निरर्वदयते । उर्दव्वं निर् अस्यति । एषा वै रुद्रस्य॒ दिक् । स्वाया॑म् ए॒व दि॒श रु॒द्रं नि॒रव॑दयते । अप उप॑ स्पृशति मेध्यत्वायं । नान्व् ईक्षेत । यद् अ॒न्वीत ॥९॥ चक्षु॑र् अस्य प्र॒मायुकः स्यात् । तस्मान् नान्वीक्ष्यः ।

> “अपपरो॒ माहू, रात्रियै मा पाह्य एषा तें मग्ने समित् तया सम् Together with this (Pravargya vessel) he makes him whom he hates go to uselessness.

> *"For Pūṣan Śaras*,121 svāhā*!"* he says. The deities that are entitled to the oblation, to them he offers it (sc. the woodsplinter anointed with clarified butter.). *“To the pressing* stones, svāhā*!”* he says. The sounds that are in the air, (8) to them he offers it. “To the *promoters*, 22 *svāhā!”* he says. The promoting gods are actually the prāṇas. To them he offers it. *"To heaven and earth*, *svāhā*!” he says. To heaven and earth he offers it. *“To the fathers* who protect the *Gharma*, svāhā*!”* he says. The Gharina-drinking fathers are actually those who were sacrificers. To them he offers it. (9)

> "To *Rudra, whose Hotṛ*-*s* are *the Rudras*, *svāhā!*" he says. Rudra he brings to fulfilment with a portion. He anoints (the splinter) on all sides. On all sides he satisfies123 Rudra. He throws in the northern direction. That is indeed Rudra's direction. He satisfies Rudra in his own direction. He touches water, to acquire fitness for the sacrifice.

124 Let him not look (to the splinter that is thrown away). If he would look, (10) his eye-sight would be liable to destruction. Therefore, it should not be looked at 125 (In the evening performance :) *“You have* brought me over *the day*,

इ॑ध्य॒स्वायु॑र् मे दा, वर्चसा माञ्जीर्" इत्य् आह । आयु॑र् एवास्मिन् वर्चो दधाति ।

> “अपीपरो मा रात्रिया, अह्नों मा पाह्य, एषा तें अग्ने समित् तया सम् इ॑ध्य॒स्वायु॑र् मे दा॒ वर्च॑सा मार्" इत्य् आह । आयु॑र् ए॒वास्मन् वर्चो दधाति । “अ॒ग्निर् ज्योति॑र् ज्योति॑र् अ॒ग्निः स्वाहा॒ सूर्यो ज्योतिर् ज्योतिः सूर्यः स्वाहे "त्य् आ॑ह । य॒थय॒जुर् ए॒वैतत् ।

ब्रह्मवादिनों वदन्ति । " होत॒व्य॑म् अग्निहो॒त्रः न हो॑त॒व्या३म् ?” इति ॥१०॥ यद् यजु॑षा जुहुयात् । अय॑थापूर्वम् आहु॑ती 26 जुहुयात् । यन् न जु॑हुयात् । अ॒ग्निः परा॑ भवेत् । "भूः स्वाहे "त्य् एव हहो॑त॒व्य॑म् । यथापूर्वम् आहु॑ती 126 जुहोति । नाग्निः परां भवति ।

> "हुत ह॒विर् मधु॑ ह॒विर्” इत्य् आह । स्व॒दय॑त्य॒ ए॒वैन॑म् । “इन्द्र॑त॒मे॒ऽग्नाव्” इत्य् ओह ॥११॥ प्रा॒णो वा इन्द्र॑तमोऽग्निः । प्राण एवैनम् इन्द्र॑तमेऽग्नौ जुहोति । protect me from the *night*. *This,* o Agni, *is your kindling stick, be kindled with it. Give* me my *full* life-time, anoint me *with lustre*,” he says. He puts the full life-time and lustre in him.

(In the morning performance :) “You *have* brought me over *the night*, *protect* me from the *day*. *This*, o *Agni*, is your *kindling stick*, *be* kindled *with it*. Give me my full *life*-time, anoint me *with* lustre," he says. He puts the full life-time and lustre in him. "Agni is *light,* light is Agni, *svāhā!* The sun is *light*, *light is the sun, svāhā!”* he says. This is exactly like the yajus-formula (expresses).

The expounders of the Veda say: "Should the Agnihotra be offered, or should it not be offered?" (11) If he would offer with the yajus-formula, he would offer the two oblations not in the right succession. If he would not offer, Agni would be lost. With: “Bhūh, svāha*!”* (the Agnihotra) should be offered. He offers the two oblations in the right succession. Agni is not lost. 127 *"*The oblation has been offered, *the* sweet *oblation*," he says. He makes it sweet (with this mantra). *"In* the most *Indra-*like fire,” he says.

(12) The most Indra-like fire is actually the prāna.

128 In prāna, the most

85 “पि॒ता नो॑ ऽसि॒ मा मां हि सर्" इत्य् आ॒हाहि सायै । “अ॒श्यामं ते देव धर्म मधु॑मतो वाज॑वतः पितुमत” इत्य् ओह ["अङ्गि॑रस्वतः "] । आ॒शिष॑म् ए॒वैताम् आ शा॑स्ते । " स्वधाविनोऽशीमहि त्वा मा मां हि सीर्" इत्य् आहाहि सायै । तेज॑सा वा एते व्यू ॥ ध्यन्ते । ये प्रवर्येण चर॑न्ति । प्राव॑न्ति । तेज॑ ए॒वात्मन् दंधते ॥१२॥

संवत्स॒रं न मा॒सम् अ॑श्नीयात् । न रा॒माम् उपे॑यात् । न मृन्मयेंन पिबेत् । नास्यं राम उच्छिष्टं पिबेत् । तेर्ज एव तत् सश्यति । दे॒वा॒सुराः संयत्ता आसन् । ते देवा विजयम् उपयन्तः । विभ्राजि सौर्ये 1 । ब्रह्म सं न्य अंदधत । यत् किंचं दिवाकीर्त्यम् । तद् एतेनैव व्रतेनांगोपायत् । तस्मा॑द् ए॒तद् व्रतं 'चर्यम् । तेज॑सो गोपी॒धाय॑ । तस्मा॑द् ए॒तानि॒ यजूंषि विभ्राजः सौर्यस्येत्य् आहुः ।

"

Indra-like fire, he offers it. “You *are* our *father*, *do* not harm me,” he says, to prevent harm. *"May we eat of you*, *god Gharma, full* of honey, *full* of strength, *full* of nourishment, [of you who *are* connected with the Angirases*],*" he says. This prayer he prays. "... *of (you) full* of *svadhā.* May we eat you. *Do* not *harm* me," he says, to prevent harm. They are certainly deprived of their brilliant energy, who are engaged in the Pravargya. They eat. 129 (Thus) they put brilliant energy in their body.

(The observance to be followed when the Pravargya mantras are learnt :) (13) For a year 30 he should eat no meat. He should not approach a Śūdra woman. He should not drink from a clay vessel. A Śūdra should not drink his remnants. Thus he sharpens his brilliant energy.

The gods and the Asuras had come into conflict. The gods, going to the (decisive) contest for victory, deposited the sacred text (the Pravargya mantras) with Vibhraj, son of Sūrya. Whatever (in it) that is to be recited by day, that he protected with this very observance. Therefore, this observance should be followed, to protect the brilliant energy. Therefore, they say that these yajus-formulas belong to Vibhrāj, son of Sūrya.

(The Pravargya vessel is placed back on the throne at the end of each morning and evening-performance :) With: *"Svāhal, you* for the rays of the sun*"* in the morning, he places (the vessel and the other paraphernalia on the throne). With: "Svāhal*, you for* the *stars"* in the evening. These are indeed his deities. With them he brings this (Pravargya) to fulfilment.

### Anuvāka 9: The disposal of the Pravargya implements

The mantras of this anuvāka are TĀ 4, 11. For a description of the ritual actions see: Śrautakośa vol. 2, Sanskrit part 1:537-540; English part 1:245-264; van Buitenen, *Pravargya*: V, §1-§12.

> “धर्म या ते दिवि शुग्” इति तस्र आहु॑तीर् जुहोति ["या गय॒ छन्द॑सि । या ब्रा॑ह्म॒णे । या ह॑वि॒र्धाणें । तां त॑ ए॒तेनाव॑ य॒जे॒ स्वाहा॑ ॥ धर्म या तेऽन्तरि॑क्षे शुक् । या

। त्रैष्टु॑मे॒ छन्द॑सि । या रा॑ज॒न्ये॑ । याग्नीध्रे । तां त॑ ए॒तेनाव॑ यजे॒ स्वाहा॑ ॥ धर्म या तें पृथि॒व्याः शुक् । या जाग॑ते॒ छन्द॑सि । या वैश्यें । या सदसि । तां तं एतेनाव॑ यजे स्वाहा॑”] । छन्दो॑भिर् ए॒वास्यै॒भ्यो लो॒केभ्यः॒ शुच॒म् अव॑ यजते । इय॒त्य् अग्ने॑ जुहोति । अथेय॒त्य् अथैय॑ति । त्रय॑ इ॒मे लोकाः । एभ्य एव लोकेभ्यः शुच॒म् अव॑ यजते ॥ ।

(1) He offers the three oblations with: "0 *Gharma*, the scorching heat of yours in heaven, *[the* one which is in the gayatri-metre, the one *which is in the Brahmins*, *the* one *which* is in the *Havirdhāna shed* *- that (scorching* heat) of *you* I sacrifice *away*, svāha*!*

O *Gharma,* the *scorching heat* of *yours* in the firmament, *the* one *which* is *in the tristubh-*metre, the one which is in *the Kṣatriyas*, *the* one *which* is *in the Agnīdhra shed* - *that* (scorching heat) *of yours I sacrifice* *away*, *svāhā*!

*O Gharma,* the scorching *heat of yours* on the *earth, the* one *which is* in *the* *jagati*-metre, *the* *one which* is in the *Vaisyas*, *the* one *which* is in *the Sadas shed* - that (scorching heat) *of yours* I *sacrifice away*, svāhā*!]”*131 With the metres he sacrifices his scorching heat away from these worlds. First he offers on this height (this is said with a gesture indicating the height of the mouth).

132 And then on this height (with a gesture indicating the height of the navel); and then on this height (with a gesture indicating the height of the knees). Three are these worlds. From these worlds he sacrifices his scorching heat away.

शिरो॒ वा ए॒तद् य॒ज्ञस्य॑ । यत् प्रवर्यः । असौ खलु वा आदित्यः प्रवर्यः । तं यद् दक्षिणा प्रत्यञ्चम् उदञ्चम् उद्वासर्येत् । जिह्मं य॒ज्ञस्य॒ शिरों हरेत् । प्राञ्चम् उद् वांसयति । पु॒रस्ता॑द् ए॒व य॒ज्ञस्य शिरः प्रति दधाति ॥२॥ प्राञ्चम् उद् वा॑सयति । तस्मा॑द् अ॒साव् आ॑दि॒त्यः पुरस्ताद् उद् ऐति । शफोपयमान् धवित्रा॑णि धृष्टी इत्य्

> *"May* Anumati *(*‘Permission*') today* for us"; *["You*, o *Anumati"]*,133 he says, for permission. “You for the protection 134 of heaven *[and* of the *firmament* - protect *our bodies* - *you*, *with* the *supporting* of the *earth we will* follow for a newer *propitious* progress*],"* he says. For the sake of heaven he supports these worlds. *"You* for *the* protection *of* the Brahmins [*and* of the *Ksatriyas* - protect our *bodies* - you*, with* the *supporting of the Vaisyas we will* follow for a *newer propitious* progress*],*" he says. (2) In these worlds he supports the people. *“You* for *the protection* of the *life-* breath *fand* of the *eye-sight*-protect our *bodies* - you, *with* the *supporting* of hearing we *will follow for* a newer *propitious progress.],*" he says. He supports the prānas in the people.

That Pravargya is actually the head of the sacrifice. Indeed, the Pravargya is yonder Āditya (the sun). If he would dispose of this (Pravargya) in the south, in the west or in the north, he would take the head of the sacrifice in the wrong way. 135 (But) he disposes of it in the east.

In the east he puts back the head of the sacrifice. (3) He disposes of it in the east. Therefore, yonder Āditya (the sun) rises in the east. The tongs and supporting vessel, the fans, the two pokers etc., they lay down with it. (The

[[P90]]

अ॒न्वव॑हरन्ति । सात्मा॑नम् एवैन सत॑नुं करोति । सात्मा॒मुष्मि॑न् लो॒के भ॑वति । य ए॒वं वेद॑ । औदुंम्बराणि भवन्ति । ऊर्गु वा उदुम्बरः । ऊर्जम् ए॒वाव॑ रुन्धे । वर्त्मना वा अन्वित्यं ॥३॥ यज्ञः रक्षा सि जिघासन्ति । साम्नां प्रस्तान्ववै॑ति । साम॒ वै रक्षोहा । रक्षसाम् अप॑हत्यै । त्रिर् निधनम् उपैति । त्रयं इमे लोकाः । एभ्य एव लोकेभ्यो॒ रक्षा\* स्य् अप॑ हन्ति । पुरुषः पुरुषो निधनम् उपैति । पुरुषः पुरुषो हि रक्षस्वी । रक्षसाम् अपहृत्यै ॥४॥

यत् पृ॑थि॒व्याम् उ॒द्वसये॑त् । पृथि॒वीः शुचार्पयेत् । यद् अ॒प्सु । अ॒पः शु॒चार्पयेत् । यद् ओष॑धीषु । ओष॑धीः शुचापयेत् । यद् वनस्पति॑िषु । वन॒स्पती॑ञ् छुचार्पयेत् । हिर॑ण्यं नि॒धायोद् बा॑सयति । अ॒मृतं॒ वै हिर॑ण्यम् ॥५॥ अ॒मृत॑ ए॒वैनं॒ प्रति॑ ष्ठापयति । “व॒ल्गुर् अ॑सि श॑यु॒धा॑या॒” इति॒ त्रिः प॑रिषिञ्चन् पर्य् ऐति [" शिशुर् जन॑धायाः "] । त्रिवृद् वा अग्निः । यावन् एवाग्निः । तस्य॒ शुचं शमयति । त्रिः पुनः पर्य ऐति । षट्

"

34 Adhvaryu) supplies it with its body, with its bodily form. He who knows thus has his body136 in yonder world. (The implements) are made of Udumbara-wood. The Udumbara is actually food. He gains food.

During its course (4) the Raksases want to destroy the sacrice. The Prastotṛ follows with the sāman.137 The sāman certainly destroys the Rak-sases. To ward off the Raksases. The concluding passage (of the sāman) he sings three times. These worlds are three. From these worlds they ward off the Rakṣases. Each person joins in the concluding passage, for each person has Raksases. (Each person sings) to ward off the Raksases.

(5) If he would dispose of it on the earth, he would afflict the earth with suffering. If in the waters, he would afflict the waters with suffering.

If on the herbs, he would afflict the herbs with suffering. If on the trees, he would afflict the trees with suffering. He lays down a piece of gold and disposes of it (on that). Gold is actually immortal. (6) He places it on the immortal.138 With: "*You* are *lovely,* beneficent *[*young *and the* keeper of *people],*" he circumambulates three times while sprinkling. Agni is actually threefold (as fire, lightning and sun). They soothe the scorching heat of Agni, as far as he extends. He circumambulates again three *

### Anuvāka 10: Śukriya sāmans and yajus-formulas; places for the disposal

This anuvāka deals first with the Pravargya sāmans and yajus-formulas and one of their names: Śukriya. Next, some optional places for the disposal of the Pravargya implements are mentioned. See: Śrautakośa vol. 2, English part 1:252, 255, 257, 260; van Buitenen, Pravargya: V. §9 and Appendix III, §201.

प्र॒जाप॑तिं वै दे॒वाः शुक्रं पर्योऽदुहन् । तद् ए॑भ्यो॒ न व्य् अभवत् । तद् अ॒ग्निर् व्य् अकरोत् । तानि शुक्रियाणि सामान्य् अभवन् । तेषां यो रसोऽत्यक्षरत् । तानि शुक्रयजू व्य् अभवत् । शुक्रियाणां वा ए॒तानि॒ शुक्रियाणि । सामपयसं वा एतयोर् अ॒न्यत् । दे॒वाना॑म् अ॒न्यत् पर्यः । यद् गोः पर्यः ॥१९॥ ततु साम्नः पर्यः । यद् अजायै पर्यः । तद् दे॒वानां॒ पय॑ः । तस्मा॒द् यत्रैतैर् यजु॑र्भश् चर॑न्ति । तत् पय॑सा चरन्ति । प्र॒जाप॑तिम् ए॒व तद् दे॒वान् पय॑सा॒न्नाद्ये॑न॒ सम् अ॑र्धयन्ति । ए॒ष ह॒ त्वै सा॒क्षात् प्र॑व॒र्ग्यं भक्षयति । यस्यै॒वं वि॒दुष॑ः प्रव॒र्ग्यः प्रवृज्यते॑ ।

150 उत्तरवेद्याम् उद् वासयेत् तेजस्कामस्य । तेजो वा उत्तरवेदिः ॥२॥ तेजः ।

(1) The gods milked something bright (*Sukra*) as milk from Prajāpati.

151 It did not suffice for them (in its undeveloped state). Agni differentiated it.

152 The Śukriya 153 sāmans came into being. Their essence that overflowed became these Sukra-yajus-formulas. Indeed, from the Śukriya (sāmans) are these Śukriya (yajus-formulas). One of these two is actually the sāman-milk. The other milk belongs to the gods. The cow's milk (2) is the milk of the sāman. The goat's milk is the milk of the gods.

154 Therefore, when they act with these yajus-formulas, then they act with milk. Thus they bring Prajāpati and the gods to fulfilment with milk, with food. And, certainly, he actually partakes of (the fruit of) the Pravargya, for whom knowing thus the Pravargya is performed.

(Optional places for the disposal of the Pravargya implements:) Let him dispose on the Uttaravedi for a (sacrificer) who desires brilliant energy. The Uttaravedi is actually brilliant energy. (3) The Pravargya is

यं द्विष्यात् । यत्र स स्यात् । तस्य दिश्य् उद् वासयेत् । एष वा अग्निर् वैश्वानरः । यत् प्रवर्ग्यः । अग्निनैवैनं वैश्वानरेणाभि प्र वर्तयति ।

brilliant energy. With brilliant energy he brings brilliant energy to fulfilment. Let him dispose on the Uttaravedi for a (sacrificer) who desires food. That Pravargya is actually the head of the sacrifice. The Uttaravedi is the mouth. He puts the mouth together with the head for food. He becomes an eater of food.

Certainly, when birds sit around his Pravargya laying disposed,155 in that (same) year birds sit around his children. (4) Therefore, he should dispose on the Uttaravedi, to protect his children.

Let him dispose either in front or at the rear (of the Uttaravedi). In the east, indeed, that Light rises. This goes down in the west. He disposes of it along its own source.

Let him dispose in the midst of waters. From the midst of the waters, indeed, that Light was born. The Pravargya is the Light. He fixes it firmly in its own source. (5)

Where he is whom he hates, let him dispose in that direction. That Pravargya is actually AgniVaiśvānara. Agni Vaiśvānara he throws at him.

Let him dispose on a branch of the Udumbara-tree. The Udumbara is actually food. Prāna is food. The Gharma is scorching heat. (6) *“*Here *I* singe the *prāna* of so-and-*so*, descendant of so-and-*so with* scorching heat,*”* he says. He singes his prāna with scorching heat. Quickly (that enemy) falls in mischief.

Where the Darbha-grass is covered all over with white ants,156 there he should dispose of the Pravargya for one who is desirous of rain. This Darbha-grass is actually "Discharger of the Waters"157 by name. Yonder Āditya (the sun) actually makes the rain rise from this (earth). For him (the sacrificer) yonder Āditya (the sun) discharges the rain. These discharged waters come (together) with the dry soil.

### Anuvāka 11: The different names of the Pravargya Ritual

In this anuvāka we find different names of the Pravargya, in order to praise it according to one commentator (S), for the sake of the performance of expiations *(prāyaścittārtha*) according to the other (B). The text of this anuvāka gives no indication that the different names, given to the Pravargya in the successive stages of the performance, were closely connected with expiation rites like e.g. those of SB 12, 6, 1 (discussed by J. Gonda in Soma's Metamorphoses (The Identifications in the Oblatory Rites *of* Śatapatha- *Brāhmaṇa* 12, 6, *1),* Amsterdam-Oxford-New York, 1983). It seems better to compare the identifications here with the names of the Agnihotra in its subsequent stages. There is a clear parallelism between the second series of identifications and those of the Agnihotra (see note 157 below),

प्र॒जाप॑तिः संभ्रयमाणः । संराट् संभृतः । धर्मः प्रवृ॑क्तः । महावीर उद्धांसितः । अ॒सौ खलु वावैष आ॑दि॒त्यः । यत् प्र॑व॒र्ग्यः । स ए॒तानि॒ नामा॑न्य॒ अकुरुत । य ए॒वं वेद॑ । विदुर् ऐनं नाम्नां । ब्रह्मवादिनों वदन्ति ॥१॥ “यो वै वसीयासं यथानामम् उपचर॑ति । पुण्या॑र्तिं वै स तस्मै कामयते । पुण्यांर्तिम् अस्मै कामयन्ते । य एवं वेदं” । तस्मा॑द् ए॒वं वि॒द्वान् । "धर्म" इति दिवा चंक्षीत । “संराड्” इति नक्त॑म् । ए॒ते वा ए॒तस्य॑ प्रि॒ये त॒नुवो॑ । ए॒ते

(1) Prajāpati (is its name) 158 when it is being collected. Samrat when collected. Gharma when placed on the fire. Mahāvīra when laying disposed.

159 This Pravargya is actually yonder āditya (the sun). He made these names for himself. He who knows thus, him they know by (his good) name.

The expounders of the Veda say: (2) “He who pays reverence to a superior person with a suitable name, he certainly wishes him obtainment160 of merit. People wish obtainment of merit to him who knows thus.” Therefore, let him who knows thus call (the Pravargya)

### Anuvāka 12: Metamorphoses of the Pravargya

In this final anuvāka we have a series of identifications of the Pravargya performance during twelve successive days. The number twelve symbolizes the twelve-fold Year, often homologized with the sun. The last paragraph connects the Pravargya once more with the sun or Agni in the form of the sun.

सविता भूत्वा प्रथमेऽन् प्र वृ॑ज्यते । तेन काम: एति । यद् द्वितीयेऽर्हन् प्रवृज्यते॑ । अ॒ग्निर् भू॒त्वा दे॒वान् ए॑ति । यत् तृतीयेऽहि॑न् प्रवृज्यते॑ । वा॒युर् भू॒त्वा प्रा॒णान् ऐति । यच् चतुर्थेऽहि॑न् प्रवृज्यते॑ । आ॒दि॒त्यो भूत्वा र॒श्मीन् ए॑ति । यत् प॑ञ्चमेऽहि॑न् प्रवृज्यते॑ । च॒न्द्रमा॑ भूत्वा नक्ष॑त्राण्य् एति ॥ १॥ यत् षष्ठेऽह॑न् प्रवृज्यते॑ । ऋ॒तुर् भूत्वा सं॑वत्स॒रम् ऐति । यत् स॑प्त॒मेऽहि॑न् प्रवृज्यतें । धा॒ता भूत्वा शक्रीम् एति । यद् अ॑ष्ट॒मेऽहि॑न् प्रवृ॑ज्यते॑ । बृह॒स्पति॑र् भूत्वा गा॑य॒त्रीम् ऐति । यन् न॑व॒मेऽहि॑न् प्रकु॒ज्य॑ते॑ ।

(1) Having become Savitr, it is placed on the fire on the first day.

Therewith, it reaches the desires (of the sacrificer).

When it is placed on the fire on the second day, having become Agni it reaches the gods.

When it is placed on the fire on the third day, having become Vāyu it reaches the breaths.

When it is placed on the fire on the fourth day, having become Āditya it reaches the rays.

When it is placed on the fire on the fifth day, having become the Moon, it reaches the stars.i⁶⁷

(2) When it is placed on the fire on the sixth day, having become the Season it reaches the year.

When it is placed on the fire on the seventh day, having become Dhātṛ it reaches the śakvari - metre.

When it is placed on the fire on the eighth day, having become Bṛhaspati it reaches the gāyatrī-metre.

When it is placed on the fire on the ninth day, having become Mitra, it reaches these threefold worlds.

When it is placed on the fire on the twelfth day, having become Soma it reaches the soma-pressing.168 Inasmuch as it is placed on the fire before the Upasads, therefore (the Pravargya/sun) goes from here directed thither, shining upon yonder worlds. ## Notes to the Text and Translation

[[P105]]

### Anuvāka 1

1 "Good fortune to us ... may it leave us not!" refers to the lengthy posterior śānti recitation (TĀ 4, 41; transl. van Buitenen, *Pravargya*:120-123). It is recited each day after the study of the Pravargya mantras during the Avāntaradīkṣā.
